# B41 — Reconcile documentation and close the complete capability ledger

Rank **41** · **P2** · **Required** · Implementation **Todo**, acceptance **pending** · Cross-cutting acceptance.

This file is a standalone implementation brief. Its requirements, common invariants, task specifications, exact applicable schema methods, dependencies, source locations, model assignment, parallel boundaries and validation are embedded below. No earlier conversation or separate fix-plan reading is needed to understand the work. Normal source inspection, mandatory current repository instructions and actual prerequisite/runtime evidence remain necessary. All code and API additions marked proposed/NEW still need implementation.

## Outcome and scope

Every required unit and genuine-gap finding passes its full specification and local/Crew/device/platform/live acceptance; baseline regressions remain green. No outstanding acceptance item, stub, fallback or documentation-only claim counts as global closure.

**Why this rank:** Final closure requires every workflow; documentation and evidence collection begin at kickoff rather than waiting for final rank.

**Primary plan units:** H20. **Additional contribution:** None.

**Execution boundary:** Implement the primary units and only this package’s stated contributions to shared units. A copied shared/dependency specification provides context and interface obligations, not ownership of all another package’s features. Record joint acceptance rather than dropping requirements or duplicating workflows.

## Model assignment

| Role | Model | Reasoning | Beyond Luna? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Author | gpt-6-luna | high | No |
| Independent reviewer | gpt-6.1-sol | high | Yes |

Final reconciliation requires skeptical cross-layer review of all findings, methods and live acceptance evidence. These are risk-based scheduling requirements for this backlog, not measured model limitations. Luna owns implementation on Luna-assigned items, working in bounded contract/adapter/native/test subtasks against the embedded specification. For Sol-owned items, Luna can implement settled subtasks while Sol owns the coupled design and integration. Escalate new authority/crypto/role decisions or unresolved parser/scope invariants to Sol rather than guessing.

Start with the assigned Luna or Sol author. Escalate Luna to Sol high when a specified contract cannot be implemented without redesign, evidence conflicts, an authority/lifecycle race remains unresolved, or two substantive fixes fail the same decisive check; provide a minimal reproduction, attempted fixes and current patch. Sol can resolve the difficult part and return bounded implementation to Luna. Astra is an exceptional last resort only if documented Sol attempts and review demonstrate a specific remaining capability need; never schedule or escalate to Astra solely because the item is security-sensitive or complex. Preserve evidence and continue independent work.

Model guidance checked 2026-10-10: [official OpenAI model selection](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/model-selection) and [official model catalog](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/models). The per-item assignment is project judgment, not a documented requirement or measured model limitation. These Codex-family seats use native agents where available, not an external codex-shim. No agent is launched by this document.

## Repository context and working rules

This is /home/chris/git/collie, the Buckeyes22/collie Android fork. The product is an independent native Kotlin/Android Views client of the Collie HTTPS API; bridge, CLI and PWA remain for compatibility. Herdr, tmux and zellij are separate host-local adapters. Extend current working-tree behavior rather than replacing it with the PWA. All 75 gap units are mandatory. This brief carries the planning context; inspect the code normally, and check the current repository rules for later changes rather than needing conversation history or another gap report.

- Preserve existing user/audit edits. Do not reset, clean, discard or overwrite unowned changes. Use an isolated implementation worktree/checkout for concurrent writers, including the current baseline edits; do not assume a checkout of HEAD contains this dirty baseline. Coordinate a baseline snapshot/patch with the integrator before branching.
- Keep Kotlin native screens, English Android resources, TLS verification, encrypted bearer custody, scoped reads and existing write gates. No WebView, Custom Tab, TWA, JavaScript bridge, direct Android Herdr socket, Android SSH, shared PTY or arbitrary remote RPC/command dispatcher. Native identity sign-in uses the R02 external ACTION_VIEW browser flow, not an embedded browser.
- New routes/capabilities/classes in this brief are proposed implementation targets, not claims of existing support. Existing cited source is the discovery starting point; verify symbols against the current checkout. Source outranks archives. Unsupported adapters get explicit negative new capabilities and a native explanation; implement the requested function on supported targets and through Crew.
- Every new read/device-read must be explicitly classified at lead and peer. New capability absence fails closed. Preserve legacy absent-field behavior only for existing features. One current scope/generation and one shared repository/transport owns native authority.
- Mutations are one-shot with current target/prompt/plan checks; timeout is outcome unknown, not permission to retry. Persist/reconcile receipts only where supported. Product confirmations name real close/remove/install/restore/handoff effects; implementation does not require extra conversational reconfirmation within authorized scope.
- Keep public DEFAULT_ORIGIN empty. Diagnostics are opt-in and never log pairing bodies, bearer tokens, OIDC material, private keys or secret content. A bare 401/403 is not evidence of pairing revocation.
- Never overwrite /home/chris/git/collie/bin/collie or /home/chris/git/collie/web/dist. Use isolated compile/PWA outputs; do not run ordinary live build/update/restart actions on the user’s active installation as validation. No release, deployment, issue publication or external communication is authorized by handing off this implementation brief.
- Functional native changes add an Unreleased entry in /home/chris/git/collie/ANDROID_CHANGELOG.md. Functional bridge/CLI/PWA changes add the applicable grouped Unreleased entry in /home/chris/git/collie/CHANGELOG.md, except an actual fork-PR submission follows the repository’s documented maintainer-side changelog exception. Do not bump app/server versions, release tags or /home/chris/git/collie/flake.lock for a feature task. Documentation/tests alone need no changelog entry.
- A new architecture exception needs a superseding ADR, not silent alteration of a settled decision. Native push, host administration, local operator fonts and configured proxy ingress must carry their specified ADR changes. Preserve install-kind distinctions; Herdr plugin actions and binary/package manager updates have different operator instructions.
- Use existing installed authenticated CLIs/APIs and documented supported access before requesting sign-in/credentials. Read /home/chris/agents/context/homelab-fleet.md before fleet/SSH/LAN/CI/Forgejo probing. Vaultwarden is locked by default; request BW_SESSION only if truly required and never print secrets. Missing access keeps the affected acceptance row pending; continue every unaffected branch.
- Run meaningful feature checks and required repository gates. Local/Crew, unsupported/mixed versions, API26/current Android, large fonts/TalkBack/narrow screen, lifecycle/process death and real controlled behavior are part of acceptance where relevant. A fixture, stub, decoder, capability bit, screenshot or safe fallback alone never closes a missing supported function.
- No direct file edit/stage/commit/revert, fictional removed pane.graphics RPC or unlimited terminal scrollback is inferred. Their actual planned equivalents are read-only Files/Changes, bounded image/observation workflows and negotiated reads plus bounded journal search.


## Prerequisites and exact handoff inputs

### B01 — Establish scoped contracts, admission and Crew dispatch

**Required interface/deliverable:**

- Implement neutral mux and host-control schemas, native models and scoped cancellation; retain legacy compatibility only for existing capabilities.
- Classify every proposed read/device-read route in lead and peer dispatch; implement typed refusal provenance, declared administration gates and one-shot operation outcomes.
- Publish shared operation/plan interfaces and required ADR changes; integrate genuine refresh through B03 and launch receipt persistence through B02.

**Minimum behavior to verify:** Contract/native fixtures prove wrong-scope cancellation, read-factor parity, capability absence refusal, redaction and ambiguous-write behavior; controlled local/Crew checks include an unsupported or older peer. C01 closes with B03 as well.

**Expected handoff:** `/home/chris/git/collie/.project-docs/implementation-evidence/capability-backlog/B01/handoff.json`. Required fields: source_revision_or_patch, exported_contracts, capabilities_and_gates, scope_and_error_semantics, changed_files, tests_with_results, remaining_acceptance. Interface readiness, not all upstream joint acceptance. Inspect current code if the handoff does not yet exist; do not infer readiness from a Todo flag.

### B02 — Persist launch intents and reconcile uncertain outcomes

- Persist device-scoped intent IDs and payload binding before launch; preserve IDs for deliberate replay.

## Parallel execution and edit ownership

**Earliest implementation wave:** 9. Waves are dependency layers, not dates or a requirement to wait for every peer in the previous wave. Start as soon as this item’s own required interfaces and gates are ready.

**Can develop concurrently in isolated worktrees:** No independent implementation package; use kickoff evidence/documentation preparation in parallel.

**Private-module work can share a checkout with these dependency-independent packages:** None. Shared integration files below still require one writer.

**Do not concurrently edit the same-checkout feature modules with:** No additional feature-module conflicts. Module leases: own feature modules only.

Private modules/tests can run concurrently after prerequisite interface readiness. Shared entrypoints/types/ADRs/docs/changelogs require one integrator or an explicit file/hunk lease; never have two agents edit the same shared file concurrently. Isolated branches may carry feature-scoped patches to these files for reviewed serial integration. Cross-package integration and live resource leases still apply.

### Common transport, admission, refusal, Crew and verification rules

#### Shared contract and verification rules

**G1 — scoped transport.** Use [/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/network/CollieApiClient.kt:739](/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/network/CollieApiClient.kt) and [/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/data/CollieRepository.kt:152](/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/data/CollieRepository.kt). Proposed `ScopedRequestKey` binds canonical HTTPS origin including mount, connection generation, paired-device identity, lead epoch, host, session and subject. Cancel/discard responses on any mismatch; never attach bearer credentials to content-supplied origins. No redirects, automatic mutation retries, forged proxy identity, or browser-cookie reuse. Read-only routing is not authorization to operate a terminal.

**G2 — admission and refusal provenance.** Existing dispatcher gates are authoritative at [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/server.ts:5969](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/server.ts). Every API except health/pair needs pairing. `read` is paired read access; `write` additionally checks Origin and the independent configured proxy/device factor; `device-read` checks that factor for Files too. Terminal/creation/rule actions require current successful snapshot-derived write authority. Preferences retain actual server read admission and show bridge-wide effects. Parent R02 adds typed failure provenance/factor (`collie/pairing`, `gateway/identity`, `collie/device`, `unknown`); propagate it through API/repository/media/offline/push. A bare HTTP401/403 never proves pairing was revoked. Only authenticated authoritative Collie expiry/revoke/unpaired evidence retires pairing and destroys retained content. Gateway identity401/403 freezes terminal writes and requests native gateway reauthentication while preserving the pairing credential and explicitly saved read content. Missing device factor removes write/Files authority, not pairing. Unknown provenance fails safe by freezing writes and withholding live protected reads without destructive wipe. Distinguish400 malformed,404 unknown versus unsupported,409 conflict and `ok:false` structured failure; preserve unknown code server prose. Every task's refusal handling inherits this rule. Tests must cover equal status codes from each source, hostile gateway bodies, revoke from authoritative source, and media/scope cancellation without accidental token/content destruction.

**G3 — crew readiness.** [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/forward.ts:52](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/forward.ts) defines forwarding, not an Android assumption. Existing allow list at line 64 includes Changes, Files/image, blobs, launches, folders, recipes and runs. It excludes Files/exist, `/api/refresh`, worktrees and lead-owned notifications/machines/cache catalog. C01 and C08 deliberately add the first two where missing; worktree remains a lead-local operation like current PWA unless the Herdr/master plan adds a separately specified peer contract. All new crew wire shape is additive optional plus `/home/chris/git/collie/CREW_PROTOCOL.md`, or an explicit protocol-version decision. Mixed versions never quietly change host or lose a request ID.

**G4 — actual verification.** Run meaningful feature tests in an isolated checkout/output directory; never overwrite `/home/chris/git/collie/bin/collie` or `/home/chris/git/collie/web/dist`. Test real protocol fixtures, wrong-scope/admission/ambiguous outcomes and security bounds, not just implementation-shaped getters. Each task needs signed-APK acceptance on a controlled harmless session, with relevant API26/current Android, lifecycle and accessibility checks. Emulator coverage supplements physical-device acceptance. Record unavailable environments once and retain those acceptance rows pending. Existing foreground monitoring/reply/STT/update/crew/device-management behavior gets regression coverage, not gratuitous replacement. Respect existing repository checkers and required gates; update documentation/changelog in the eventual functional change, not this planning artifact.

Every asynchronous result is discarded after identity/generation changes. Keep crew routing in the neutral bridge API; a host is not a replacement origin. Use separate read admission (ordinary paired read for Changes; `device-read` for Files only) and existing device-write admission for terminal/structural mutations. Registration/preference writes use the server's device-owned admission, not invented terminal-write requirements. Decode additive unknown display fields safely; refuse unknown mutation enums, unknown prompt recipes and unrecognized notification schemas. Surface unauthorized, proxy-auth-required, unsupported older server, temporarily unavailable and not-found distinctly. Never interpret a 403 or disabled capability as empty data.

Saved copies cannot authorize reply/keys, prompt actions, uploads, launch/worktree/rename/close, recipe changes, notification enrollment, or server updates. Require a successful current scoped refresh before enabling writes again. Classify refusals by authenticated response source and factor, not HTTP status alone. Authoritative Collie paired-device expiration/revocation (its exact declared code/source) invalidates actors and wipes the pairing credential, sensitive stored content and registrations/notifications; origin replacement clears its read stores and registration. R02 gateway/issuer missing or expired identity invalidates only ProxyIdentityStore and freezes actions for native reauthentication while retaining a still-valid Collie pairing bearer. Unknown or unclassified 401/403 freezes actions and displays the failing access state without destructive credential wipe. Do not let a proxy HTML/body containing "device expired" impersonate Collie revocation. Add typed source/factor fields to ApiFailure/contract models; configured gateway responses mark their own factor and sanitize upstream provenance, while genuine Collie response/error codes are preserved explicitly. Optional pin identity and display preferences may survive without content. ViewModels own screen work; Activities observe lifecycle and restore lightweight navigation/form identity using SavedStateHandle, never bearer tokens or transcript bodies in Bundle. Follow [Android activity lifecycle](https://developer.android.com/guide/components/activities/activity-lifecycle), [saved ViewModel state](https://developer.android.com/topic/libraries/architecture/viewmodel/viewmodel-savedstate), and [Keystore](https://developer.android.com/privacy-and-security/keystore).

### Neutral control, capability, operation and Crew contract

#### Shared implementation contract: H01 is a dependency of every new action

**All routes below marked NEW are proposed Collie contracts, not existing Herdr or Collie APIs.** Prefix every concrete HTTP request with the configured Collie mount. Native always talks HTTPS plus pairing bearer to Collie. Neither Android nor a crew link carries raw Herdr RPC, terminal endpoint bytes, SSH, a browser runtime or an arbitrary RPC dispatcher. The owning Collie calls local Herdr or documented host-local CLI primitives. The mux adapter remains host-local; saved remote administration uses a separate host-management port, and live remote panes are reached through that machine's Collie crew API.

For ordinary pane/layout operations, extend [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/mux/types.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/mux/types.ts) and [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/mux/capabilities.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/mux/capabilities.ts) with typed neutral methods/capabilities. For integration/plugin/server/persistence/machine administration, add NEW `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/host-control/types.ts`, `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/host-control/registry.ts`, and `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/host-control/herdr.ts`; publish their capabilities separately in `/api/config`. This avoids embedding a host axis inside MuxAdapter. Each adapter must implement verified semantics or an explicit named unsupported outcome. Implement matching native methods in [/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/network/CollieApiClient.kt](/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/network/CollieApiClient.kt), serializers in NEW `/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/network/ControlModels.kt`, repository methods in [/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/data/CollieRepository.kt](/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/data/CollieRepository.kt), and actual UI callers listed per task. New flags require `supportsNew(name) == true`; missing or unknown flags fail closed. Preserve legacy `supports()` compatibility for existing actions ([/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/network/WireModels.kt:44](/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/network/WireModels.kt)). An older peer's 404 becomes a clear unsupported-feature response, never a raw RPC fallback.

NEW `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/control/contracts.ts` owns discriminated request/response schemas, positive capabilities, maximum sizes, HTTP verb/gate, scope type and outcome classification. NEW `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/control/routes.ts` is called from [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/server.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/server.ts) through its existing RouteCaller/gate/runtime resolver ([/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/server.ts:563](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/server.ts)). Every session route resolves `(host,session)` once; pane/tab/workspace IDs must belong to that runtime. Host-only routes reject irrelevant session selectors and never accidentally target a lead's primary session. Use opaque object IDs, not user-supplied disk paths; derive plugin/config/backup paths from declared server-owned records with containment, owner checks and allowlists. Files/config additions require updating the current path-boundary ADRs rather than quietly bypassing their restrictions.

Read actions keep pairing/TLS/identity/host gates. Sensitive argv, logs, config and selections use `device-read` plus response redaction. Mutations keep both existing write factors and operator phone-policy switches, verify their current target and expected state, and audit a redacted verb/target/outcome, never prompt bodies, tokens, selected secret text or credentials. Native product confirmation names the real effects for close/remove/restore/install/stop/handoff/host scroll actions; this is UI design, not an extra conversational permission gate for writing this plan.

NEW `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/control/operations.ts` and `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/control/plans.ts` extend the durable receipt pattern ([/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/launch-receipts.ts:7](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/launch-receipts.ts), [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/worktree-receipts.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/worktree-receipts.ts)). Every mutation carries a request ID and binds device, owning host/session, capability, canonical payload hash, target identity and plan evidence. Same ID/same payload replays a recorded result; reuse with a different payload refuses. Reserve before dispatch; persist completion. In-flight/pending/unknown outcomes are typed and visible through NEW `GET /api/control/operations/<id>`. A timeout or disconnected reply is not proof that a write failed. Never automatically replay a terminal send, move, install, removal or restore after uncertain dispatch; inspect authoritative state and require a new deliberate product action where effect cannot be proven. Do not promise exactly-once remote effects across a crash before a receipt. Plan tokens expire and revalidate immediately before writes; they do not authorize unrelated targets.

Crew work is mandatory for every owning-host route: update [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/forward.ts:64](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/forward.ts), [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/forward.ts:159](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/forward.ts), [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/router.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/router.ts), [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/peer-client.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/peer-client.ts), [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/admission.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/admission.ts), [/home/chris/git/collie/CREW_PROTOCOL.md](/home/chris/git/collie/CREW_PROTOCOL.md), plus response masking. **Explicitly classify every new GET/read POST as read or device-read in both lead and peer dispatch.** The current default treats non-pane routes as write and unknown pane actions as write; do not inherit this by accident. Forward only a closed allowlist, preserve scopes/device attribution, pin mutual TLS/crew secret, negotiate new feature support, and preserve outcome-unknown behavior. Make the required protocol decision: additive optional fields with closed absence semantics where possible; bump the crew version if new streaming/operation behavior is not expressible compatibly. Existing Windows crew restrictions remain published only until the mandatory tested Windows crew extension in the companion Collie plan is implemented; the standalone Windows host paths in this plan still get native screens and honest capabilities.

H01 acceptance: fixtures test pairing/device refusal, peer gate parity, malformed selectors, cross-runtime IDs, capability absence on old peers, plan expiry, body-hash mismatch, concurrent duplicate dispatch, disconnect before/after dispatch, redaction and read classification. Verify a paired Android client against a disposable solo bridge and a disposable three-node crew before any new feature is marked closed. Existing tests in [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/server.test.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/server.test.ts), [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/forward.test.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/forward.test.ts), [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/router.test.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/router.test.ts), [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/peer-client.test.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/peer-client.test.ts) must gain meaningful cases; NEW `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/control/contracts.test.ts` and `/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/test/java/com/lateapex/collie/network/ControlModelsTest.kt` must not replace the existing suites. Closure: contracts, port, adapter capability, gate, forwarding, durable operation result and native caller all exist for each claimed feature.

### Decisions and canonical shared ownership

#### Decisions applying to every implementation unit

1. Use `/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/network/CollieApiClient.kt` and `/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/data/CollieRepository.kt` for HTTPS, mount normalization, encrypted bearer custody and authoritative refusal handling. Navigation scope is `(connection generation, origin, host, session, subject identity)`; cancel requests and discard mismatched responses on scope change.
2. Preserve paired reads, snapshot-derived terminal write authority and the independent proxy/device factor. Files retains `device-read`. Metadata, plugin/session operations and external administration get declared, narrow permissions and confirmation, never an unrestricted RPC or command pass-through.
3. Writes remain one-shot. Preserve existing launch receipt IDs across a deliberate retry of the same intent; do not invent general exactly-once guarantees for upstream methods without receipts. For an uncertain mutation, reconcile authoritative state and require an explicit repeat if the result remains unknown.
4. Keep Android native: no embedded browser, direct Herdr socket, Android SSH, cross-host socket, shared PTY attach/control or raw vendor vocabulary over Crew. The missing *functions* enter neutral Collie HTTP contracts. Server-side CLI adapters use fixed executables, bounded argv and operator-declared identifiers; no arbitrary shell interpolation or client-supplied filesystem root.
5. Unsupported backends get explicit negative capability declarations and honest native refusal. Implement equivalents where a backend's official interface provides them; do not emulate support by applying Herdr keys or methods to another mux. The new feature must work fully on the target supported backend and through Crew, with mixed-version refusal covered.
6. Native push uses the concrete UnifiedPush/ntfy baseline, an authenticated registration lifecycle and origin-signed encrypted messages. The current decoder is reusable inner validation, not authentication. Provider replacement, replay/clear tombstones, device revocation and process death are mandatory acceptance, not future follow-up.
7. New user-facing native strings belong in English Android resources. Large-font, TalkBack, narrow-screen, loading, refusal and process-death behavior are part of each UI slice. Per-device font preferences never restyle agent-authored terminal/transcript text.
8. A new feature updates `/home/chris/git/collie/android/UPSTREAM_API_COMPATIBILITY.md`, `/home/chris/git/collie/android/README.md` and applicable changelogs/ADRs. Server/CLI/Crew changes retain repository versioning and wire-change checks. This planning turn changes no versions and deploys nothing.

#### Reconciled owners and shared modules

The overlapping C/A/H IDs are layer specifications for one implementation. Use the following canonical choices; add facades only when they share the same underlying state/contract.

| Workflow | Canonical contract/module owner | Integration requirement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Scope and admission | C01/A00/H01; native `ScopedRequestKey`/read context and existing repository | One connection generation and typed source/factor refusal model. Changes is paired read; Files is device-read. |
| Refresh/read ceilings | H02–H04 plus C01/A13 | One declared read-limit schema and owning-runtime refresh path, including explicit Crew read forwarding. |
| Journal/file media | C03/C06–C08/A02/A05 with H19 producer equivalents | One scoped bounded media loader; content-supplied URLs never receive credentials. |
| New/folders/worktrees/recipes/runs | C09–C14/A03/A04/A06/A07; H08 adds removal | One New workflow; current lead-local worktree API gains new peer operations only through H01/H08's expressly designed neutral contract. |
| Offline retention | C24 `OfflineContentStore` facade plus A14 `RetainedReadDatabase` backing | New dashboard/mirror/transcript persistence Off by default. Enabled transcript policy is 1 or 7 days; dashboard/mirror <=24 hours only while enabled. Existing separately opted encrypted draft retention <=48 hours never persists password input and is not enabled by the read cache. |
| Push | C21/A15 | One signed v2 protocol, strict P-256 DER signature framing, device-owned lifecycle, shared golden fixtures and five event channels. |
| Typeface/operator fonts | C25/A16 | Bounded local WOFF2-to-SFNT decoder; one per-device preference and same-origin declared-font store. |
| Local settings/pins/keypad | C25/C26/A19 | One versioned preference store and complete board import/export/editor, queue lifetime unchanged. |
| Entry/QR/proxy login | C27/A20 and R02 | One exported `OpenLinkActivity` dispatches to internal import/sign-in screens using a single parser and transaction store; configured-host App Links addon is implemented and tested, alongside universal confirmed import. |
| APK updates | C28/A21 `AndroidReleaseRepository`, `AndroidUpdatesActivity`, bounded download worker | Extend the existing `/home/chris/git/collie/scripts/android-release.sh`, its test and `/home/chris/git/collie/.github/workflows/android-release.yml` outputs (`collie-android-$version.apk`, `release-metadata.json`, SHA256SUMS) with a detached signed metadata sidecar; one integrity contract and existing-signer PackageInstaller workflow. |
| Machine alerts and deputy | C19/C20/C23 with C21 and W01 | Implement state/checkpoint schemas first; activation/delivery/takeover integrate after them. Keep histories, alert revisions and registration generations honest across role transitions. |

For the ntfy baseline, implement the documented private-instance ACL: default deny-all, distributor-authenticated subscription, and anonymous write-only access limited to the registered unpredictable `up` topic (or the documented UnifiedPush prefix where explicitly configured). UnifiedPush requires application-server anonymous write; do not depend on an unsupported publisher credential flow. RFC8291 encryption and the verified Collie signature protect message content/authenticity; topic secrecy alone does not. See [ntfy UnifiedPush configuration](https://docs.ntfy.sh/config/).

Push's installation-proof-only retirement endpoint outside `/api` is a proposed narrowly scoped exception requiring a new ADR, strict signature/registration generation/replay counter checks and rate limits. It may only retire its own installation and cannot read, enroll, renew, alter pairing or operate a terminal. It closes offline/proxy-blocked retirement; a bounded registration lease remains required. No implementation should silently exempt existing `/api` routes from pairing to create it.

## Complete acceptance specifications for final review

The final reviewer owns reconciliation of every unit. All 75 detailed unit requirements are embedded here so final review does not depend on reading other backlog items or prior plans. This is review scope, not an instruction to reimplement other owners’ work. Compare each against its source patch, test results, live evidence and handoff.

### Final review requirement — C21

#### C21 — implement the complete native push provider and bridge contract

Current: E22; `/home/chris/git/collie/android/NATIVE_PUSH_CONTRACT.md:3` is only pure decoder/provider seams; line 86 lists absent integration. Browser `/api/subscribe` is implemented but does not register native receivers. Proposed below is Collie's design, not a claim about UnifiedPush's security guarantees.

Chosen baseline: official UnifiedPush Android connector plus operator-configured self-hosted ntfy distributor. FCM is a documented comparison/future provider, not a selection blocker or required Google account. Pin reviewed connector version and transitive artifacts at execution (official Maven Central candidate 3.3.5; do not silently assume future KDoc matches pin), use current non exported PushService integration and official caller/token/ACK validation. No permanent foreground polling service. Operator configures ntfy/distributor availability and bridge egress by CLI/config, never app credential form. Create `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/native-push/{provider,registrations,envelope,dispatcher}.ts`, tests beside them, and proposed `/home/chris/git/collie/cli/native-push.ts`. Add contracts to server/types, integrate existing notification event producers `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/notifications.ts`, `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/push.ts`, `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/notify.ts` through one provider seam; maintain browser delivery unchanged. ADR amendment explicitly authorizes only configured encrypted event egress and no arbitrary remote content.

**Registration contract, lead-owned without crew forwarding.** GET `/api/native-push/config` is paired read,200 `{enabled,providers:[{id:"unifiedpush",allowedOrigin}],signingKeyId,serverVerificationKey,vapidPublicKey,envelopeVersion:2}`; it exposes no publisher credential or registered endpoints. POST `/api/native-push/registrations` takes `{installationId,installationPublicKey,endpoint,p256dh,auth,provider:"unifiedpush",generation,appVersion}`; paired read admission plus explicit normalized Origin verification applies, without terminal-write authority. Derive owner from the pairing registry and R02 ingress binding, never client identity fields.201 returns `{registrationId,challengeId,expiresAt,signingKeyId,serverVerificationKey,state:"pending"}`. Deliver a signed encrypted one-use challenge to the connector. POST `/api/native-push/registrations/:id/confirm` takes `{challengeId,nonce,installationProof}` and returns200 active only after paired-owner/Origin/generation/challenge checks and P-256 installation signature validation. DELETE `/api/native-push/registrations/:id` requires paired owner and Origin, returning204 retired. Unknown or another owner's ID404; expired challenge409; malformed400; admission failures use G2. Atomically replace an older installation generation, allow <=5 active installations per paired device, expire pending challenges after10 minutes, and store endpoint secrets with mode0600/Windows ACL equivalent. The installation key proof is a proposed Collie addition, not a UnifiedPush guarantee. Pin the server key from the paired HTTPS enrollment response. Enrollment public keys are base64url without padding containing DER SubjectPublicKeyInfo for P-256; reject any other curve/key type. RFC8291 `p256dh` is base64url of the65-byte uncompressed P-256 point and `auth` of16 random bytes, validated before provider use. Confirmation signs uint32-BE length-prefixed strict UTF-8 domain `CollieNativePushConfirmV1`, registrationId, canonical generation, challengeId and nonce; renewal uses domain `CollieNativePushRenewV1`, registrationId, generation and fresh nonce. The nonce is32 cryptographically random bytes, base64url without padding, bound to owner/registration/generation and consumed atomically. Neither proof domain can be replayed as a notification or retirement signature. Rotate it through a signed old-key transition plus authenticated refresh; push never establishes trust in an unknown key.

**Lease and offline retirement.** An active registration has a finite 30-day lease, renewed during an authenticated foreground connection by `POST /api/native-push/registrations/:id/renew` with `{nonce,installationProof}`. A paired-owner GET `/api/native-push/registrations/:id` returns state, lease expiry and a one-use challenge nonce; renewal verifies the installation signature, owner, generation and Origin before extending the lease. Local disconnect commits active=false before token/content removal, unregisters the distributor and cancels notifications; it attempts the ordinary authenticated DELETE with a bounded timeout while the credential is still in memory. For an offline unpair, retain only a signed retirement request, never a bearer, endpoint or notification text. Proposed narrow `POST /native-push/retire` is outside the always-paired `/api/*` namespace and requires an explicit ADR admission decision: front-door transport/Host protections remain; body `{registrationId,generation,counter,installationProof}` is signed by the previously enrolled installation key over uint32-BE length-prefixed domain `CollieNativePushRetireV1` and those fields. The server validates the known registration/key and durable increasing counter, and can only retire that installation; it cannot register, renew, change endpoints, read secrets or grant terminal authority. Invalid proof403, malformed400, idempotent retirement204. Queue/retry only this restricted signed request. If offline or an identity proxy blocks it, local receive suppression remains immediate and server lease expiry bounds the orphan; document that residual honestly. Role takeover preserves retired markers and sequences or issues a fresh generation after authenticated enrollment.

**Endpoint security.** Operator configuration allowlists the exact normalized HTTPS origin and strict ntfy UnifiedPush path grammar; endpoint URL length is <=1000 bytes. Reject userinfo, query credentials, fragments, ambiguous escapes, encoded slashes, alternate IP forms and unapproved ports. A client cannot supply arbitrary outbound headers. Every send resolves DNS, validates the result, binds that result to the actual TLS connection with the approved SNI host, refuses redirects and enforces timeout/rate/body limits. Private IPs require an explicit operator-configured endpoint CIDR; reject loopback, link-local, metadata, unspecified and multicast addresses. The standard UnifiedPush baseline has no publisher-auth enrollment requirement. Baseline operator setup uses ntfy default deny-all, authenticated distributor subscriptions and the documented anonymous `up*` WRITE-ONLY ACL prefix. This grants neither anonymous read nor access to general-purpose topics; new unpredictable distributor topic enrollment needs no per-topic administrative credential. Collie independently allowlists each registered exact origin/topic and verifies signed encrypted challenges/messages. Narrow per-topic ACLs may be supported later by an explicit available operator CLI adapter, but are not an enrollment prerequisite for this baseline. Distributor subscribe credentials stay in the distributor and never enter Collie registration. This concrete configuration must be tested, and does not make anonymous publishing an application-authenticity guarantee. Use `X-UnifiedPush:1` and RFC8291 encryption with connector p256dh/auth; VAPID does not authenticate the Collie message to the receiver.

**Envelope and versioning.** Signature input is the concatenation of individually uint32-big-endian byte-length-prefixed fields in this exact order: strict UTF-8 domain `CollieNativePushV2`, registrationId, canonical unsigned decimal generation, slot, canonical unsigned decimal sequence, issuedAt, expiresAt, then the raw payload bytes. Decimal values have no leading zeros except zero itself. The signature is SHA256withECDSA over P-256, strict ASN.1 DER, encoded base64url without padding; payload uses base64url without padding too. Require API26 Java/Bun known-answer signing/verification fixtures. Verify original raw payload bytes; JSON is never reserialized for authenticity. JSON envelope carries these fields, keyId, base64url payload and signature. Receiver verifies signature/pinned key, pairing connection generation/registration/scope, expiry, future clock skew bound of five minutes before payload decode; expiry remains within the existing inner 24-hour maximum. Parse only the bounded strict outer envelope first. Verify its pinned key, signature and metadata before decoding any inner notification payload. Outer metadata uses canonical unsigned decimal strings for generation/sequence/issuedAt/expiresAt; constrain the corresponding inner legacy sequence to positive safe integers<=9007199254740991, its existing exact numeric safe range and compare the fields. Existing strict v1 decoder remains unchanged. Add strict v2 schema to `/home/chris/git/collie/android/NATIVE_PUSH_CONTRACT.md` and native decoder: existing blocked/done/update/clear + `machine`/`cache`, exact typed targets `home`, `pane(host,session,paneId)`, `updates`, `machine(machineId)`, `cache(host,session,paneId)`; optional absence of pane never guess last active pane. Server has corresponding golden encoders. Inner payload UTF-8<=2400 bytes, signed plaintext envelope<=3500 bytes, encrypted RFC8291 wire<=4096 bytes; shorten only display text at Unicode boundaries, never IDs/targets/signature. UnifiedPush AND_3.1.0 plaintext ceiling 3993 is not a license to ship 4096 plaintext. Registration challenge uses distinct signed payload kind not notification and never reaches UI.

**Delivery/lifecycle.** Allocate durable per-registration/per-slot monotonic sequences before publishing. Persist clear tombstones, retry transport delivery with identical signed bytes/sequence under capped backoff, and apply existing kind preferences, snooze, watches, machine policy and redaction before encoding. Clear events bypass mute so an old alert can be removed. The native SQLite transaction stores an accepted sequence and pending NotificationManager projection before displaying; restart replays a pending projection, rejects older sequences and retains clear tombstones until retirement. Authoritative Collie pairing revoke/expiry retires the registration; ordinary gateway OAuth expiry follows G2 and does not retire it. R02 provides a server-owned ingress binding store with issuer/sub/jkt, explicit operator-mapped pairedDeviceId and binding revision. Enrollment derives that binding from the trusted gateway profile; an authenticated mapped-device revocation event retires only registrations enrolled under that binding. No client supplies arbitrary mapping. A changed endpoint, installation generation, credential identity or lead epoch invalidates old registrations. An unexpected provider401/403 indicates topic ACL/configuration failure in this anonymous-write baseline and freezes delivery for operator repair; endpoint404/410 retires that endpoint, without clearing the Collie bearer. Android installation proof keys use Keystore P-256; RFC8291 key custody uses the official connector key manager. Windows state/key ACL behavior depends on W01/W02 acceptance. Tests distinguish mapping revocation from transient OAuth expiry and verify targeted retirement, not bridge-wide cancellation.

**UI/execution/tests/closure.** Android agent implements Settings enable→permission/distributor/registration lifecycle, distinct blocked/done/update/machine/cache channels, disabled permission link, exact target Pending Intents with no reply action authority, notification privacy uses redacted default and foreground coexistence. Provider foundation depends on C01 and the strict envelope/registration contract; event producers from C16/C18/C20 and exact navigation C27 join integration acceptance after that foundation. Consult D01–D05; R02 binding enrollment/revocation integration is required for final acceptance. Implement contracts/golden payloads/security tests first, server registration provider next, connector+receiver ledger next, integrated producers last. Test wrong owner/challenge/installation proof, ssrf/dns rebinding/redirects, signature wrong key/replay/expiry/oversize/sequence persist crash/clear before old alert/retire generation/mixed v1/v2, preferences/snooze/redaction, provider failures and no Google Play environment. Physical device: selected self hosted distributor, screen locked/background/process death/reboot, permission denied/reallowed, battery idle, duplicates/out of order/clears, all 5 event types, unpair/revoke and role change, API26 and API33+. Terminal condition: native background events reach exact configured device/target with verified authenticity and durable ordering, and every failure/revocation path demonstrably fails closed; provider contract, configuration docs and end-to-end device evidence must exist together.

### Final review requirement — A15

##### A15 — Real native background push (P1, required)

**Now:** pure v1 payload decoder and provider seam exist, delivery does not ([/home/chris/git/collie/android/NATIVE_PUSH_CONTRACT.md](/home/chris/git/collie/android/NATIVE_PUSH_CONTRACT.md) (line 3), [/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/notifications/NativePushPayload.kt](/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/notifications/NativePushPayload.kt) (line 39)). Implement the concrete baseline and acceptance protocol below; do not turn on unimplemented delivery via notification preferences. Crosswalk to bridge plan **C21**, with C16/C18/C20 preference/cache/machine dependencies.

**Provider decision:** UnifiedPush Android connector with operator-configured self-hosted ntfy is the baseline. It suits private/self-hosted HTTPS, avoids mandatory Google Play/Firebase/browser subscriptions, and delegates persistent transport to the distributor. Use official connector candidate `org.unifiedpush.android:connector:3.3.5`, verify published source/POM/license/compatibility before locking it; current Kotlin 2.2.21/minSdk26 are in [/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/build.gradle.kts](/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/build.gradle.kts) (line 14). Add dependency locks, license entry and release shrinker smoke. [Connector API](https://unifiedpush.org/kdoc/connector/) and [published artifact](https://central.sonatype.com/artifact/org.unifiedpush.android/connector) document the dependency. ntfy's Android distributor uses its own self-hosted instant-delivery service; no Collie long-poll foreground service is added ([ntfy phone documentation](https://docs.ntfy.sh/subscribe/phone/)). FCM was compared: it adds Google/Firebase server credentials and data-message lifecycle constraints, with priority subject to visibility/deprioritization ([FCM receive](https://firebase.google.com/docs/cloud-messaging/android/receive-messages), [FCM priority](https://firebase.google.com/docs/cloud-messaging/android-message-priority)). It is not a second required provider; keep the existing provider interface so a separately approved deployment can add one later.

**Android files:** proposed `/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/notifications/ColliePushService.kt`, `/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/notifications/PushRegistrationCoordinator.kt`, `/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/notifications/PushEnvelopeVerifier.kt`, `/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/notifications/PushLedger.kt`, `/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/notifications/PushNotificationPresenter.kt`, `/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/notifications/PushReconcileWorker.kt`, `/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/notifications/NativePushV2Payload.kt`. Keep v1 decoder unchanged and tested. Extend A00 API/repository, ConnectionStore, CollieApplication, SettingsActivity and manifest; add `/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/res/drawable/ic_notification.xml` and English channel/settings text in `/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/res/values/strings.xml`.

**Device-owned registration:** paired lead-only `GET /api/native-push/config`; `POST /api/native-push/registrations` sends installation ID, P-256 installation public key, provider unifiedpush, endpoint, RFC8291 p256dh/auth, generation and app version. Response201 is pending registration/challenge/expiry/signing-key ID+server verification key. Encrypted signed challenge arrives through the selected distributor; `POST /api/native-push/registrations/:id/confirm` sends challenge ID/nonce/install-key proof over paired HTTPS and activates only the matching device-owned install. `DELETE /api/native-push/registrations/:id` retires only its owner installation; revoking the paired device retires all its installations. Never forward these routes to Crew peers; lead owns event aggregation. Enrollment explicitly opt-in and uses paired-read owner admission plus explicit normalized Origin checks, not terminal write permission. Server config supplies allowed provider origins and version/capability; endpoint import never authorizes arbitrary outbound HTTP.

**Gateway binding lifecycle:** R02 owns the server-side `(issuer, subject, DPoP key thumbprint, pairedDeviceId, bindingRevision)` map. C21 records the verified server-derived ingress binding on enrollments using that profile. Map revocation retires its bound registrations and sends authenticated clear/retirement; a currently connected app cancels affected notifications. Test local event crash/replay and binding replacement. Transient identity-token expiry only triggers proxy reauthentication and never retires push or pairing. Direct Tailscale registrations retain their actual pairing lifecycle; no client header can claim a gateway binding. An offline device cannot be guaranteed to erase previously delivered notifications until transport resumes; required acceptance documents that limit honestly.

**Server prerequisites owned by C21:** validate exact configured HTTPS ntfy origin/port and strict unpredictable topic path; reject credentials/query/fragment, redirect, off-origin and private-address SSRF unless an operator explicitly pins the approved private host/address range. Resolve/revalidate DNS on each send and do not forward Collie bearer/cookie/Origin headers. ntfy topic capability is not sender authentication. Configure least-privilege topic access suitable for UnifiedPush, avoid broad anonymous read/write, and explicitly test binary UnifiedPush POST/header behavior ([ntfy publishing](https://docs.ntfy.sh/publish/), [ntfy configuration](https://docs.ntfy.sh/config/), [UnifiedPush security guidance](https://unifiedpush.org/developers/intro/)). Persist device/installation/generation ownership, pending challenge, active destination, server per-slot monotonic sequence, retirement and bounded retry/TTL; delivery outcomes accepted/retired/retry never imply device displayed. Pending endpoint replacement activates only after challenge, then atomically retires predecessor; finite lease and authenticated renewal bound orphan registrations.

**Authenticated payload protocol:** paired TLS response pins the lead's P-256 verification key (X.509 SubjectPublicKeyInfo DER, base64url); installation private key is Android Keystore non-exportable P-256. Separate these keys from RFC8291 encryption/VAPID keys. Server signs SHA256withECDSA over the exact domain `CollieNativePushV2`, with 32-bit unsigned big-endian byte-length prefixes for the domain and each following UTF-8/binary field: registration ID, generation, slot, sequence, issuedAt, expiresAt and raw inner payload bytes. Integers use ASCII decimal without sign/leading zeros; every field occurs in the stated order and trailing data is rejected. Envelope duplicates of registration/generation/slot/sequence/expiry must exactly match decoded inner values where present. Use shared byte fixtures for this framing; challenge proof uses a distinct length-prefixed domain and signed raw challenge body. Wire signature is strict ASN.1 DER ECDSA, **not JOSE raw r||s**; Bun and API26 Java sign/verify known-answer fixtures must agree. Reject malformed/noncanonical framing, wrong key/domain/generation, tampered fields or raw bytes before any inner decoder/UI effect. Rotation keys arrive only via authenticated paired HTTPS; an incoming message cannot install a verification key. P-256 uses Android-supported algorithms rather than assuming Ed25519 platform availability on API26 ([Android cryptography](https://developer.android.com/privacy-and-security/cryptography)).

RFC8291 encrypt the signed envelope and use official connector decryption; keep ciphertext ≤4096 bytes, signed-envelope plaintext ≤3500 and inner JSON ≤2400 **with actual serialized/base64 size checked**, not assumed from text limits. Dynamically budget UTF-8 title/body truncation; never truncate identities. If a detailed target cannot fit, use an explicitly valid home fallback for the owned registration or reject with a redacted reason. Spec AND_3.1.0 defines 4096-byte transport/3993-byte cleartext bounds and unknown connector-token rejection ([UnifiedPush Android specification](https://unifiedpush.org/developers/spec/android/)). Connector decrypts, app verifies lead signature, then selects exact negotiated v1/v2 strict decoder. V2 adds machine/cache kinds and explicit internal Machines/Cache targets; retains blocked/done/update/clear semantics, strict unknown-field refusal and safe sequence/TTL. No payload origin, token, provider URL, shell command or unrestricted URL. Diagnostic logs omit endpoint capability, plaintext, key material and challenge nonce. Verify connector key storage from pinned source; integrate its supported KeyManager with encrypted Keystore-backed private/auth storage when needed and never claim signing-key Keystore protection also protects default RFC8291 keys ([KeyManager API](https://unifiedpush.org/kdoc/connector/org.unifiedpush.android.connector.keys/-key-manager/)).

**Durability and receiver lifecycle:** subclass official PushService as non-exported; its library merged receiver handles distributor identity/token/ACK transport. Inspect merged manifest and reject direct forged service events; do not invent a custom deprecated MessagingReceiver path ([PushService API](https://unifiedpush.org/kdoc/connector/org.unifiedpush.android.connector/-push-service/)). Persist registration/slot high-water, clear tombstone and desired notification projection in one SQLite transaction **before** applying NotificationManager. Reconcile desired projection after crash so commit-before-display does not lose valid notification; no clear replay resurrects it. Encrypt retained display text; deterministic hashed tag+slot ID prevents replacement collisions. Tombstones live until the registration is irrevocably retired, not merely payload TTL. TTL ≤24h; expired, duplicate, out-of-order, future-issued and retired-generation messages do nothing. Display from received data without background HTTP. At startup/register callbacks re-register known instance, handle new endpoint, unregistered, failed and temporary-unavailable state, and retry bounded network work using [WorkManager](https://developer.android.com/develop/background-work/background-tasks/persistent); do not rely on WorkManager for instant delivery. Coordinator survives process death using durable pending state; boot/launch reconciliation observes OS unlock and distributor lifecycle.

**Permission and notification UX:** explicit Enable background notifications explains distributor installation/configuration, obtains OS distributor picker result and in-context API33+ POST_NOTIFICATIONS permission. Show provider selected/registration pending/active/permission denied/channel blocked/distributor missing/server unavailable independently. API26+ separate blocked/done/update/machine/cache channels, useful user-controlled importance, private lock-screen contents and generic-public version; clear events silently cancel. Respect areNotificationsEnabled and per-channel restrictions ([Android notification permission](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/views/notifications/notification-permission), [channels](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/views/notifications/channels)). Notification tap uses explicit immutable Activity PendingIntent with unique registration/slot identity, never a broadcast/service trampoline, and routes only decoded identities after checking active connection generation and current authorization ([PendingIntent security](https://developer.android.com/privacy-and-security/risks/pending-intent)). Missing pane navigates home with explanation; no tap performs reply/keys/update install. Doze/network/OEM and distributor battery restrictions are explicit diagnostics, not a promised fixed latency ([Doze](https://developer.android.com/training/monitoring-device-state/doze-standby)). Force-stop is an OS stopped state and is not bypassed; Android15 cancels PendingIntents and recovery begins after user interaction ([Android stopped state](https://developer.android.com/about/versions/15/behavior-changes-all#stopped-state)).

**Disconnect/revocation:** commit local inactive state before wiping bearer/cache, cancel all notifications and unregister distributor immediately. Try owned server retirement with a short bounded request while current token remains available. If offline, retain only a replay-safe restricted installation-key retirement proof (no bearer/text/endpoint), queue C21's proposed `POST /native-push/retire` restricted front-door exception outside always-paired `/api`: body registrationId/generation/counter/installationProof, signed with installation key over domain CollieNativePushRetireV1 and exact length-prefixed fields. Server validates the owned stored installation key and durable strictly increasing retirement counter; the exception can only retire, never read/activate/renew/change endpoint. Add the specific ADR/route-gate/security tests, delete proof key after acknowledgement, and store no bearer. Active registrations have finite 30-day lease renewed via owned `POST /api/native-push/registrations/:id/renew` with installationProof/nonce. If a proxy blocks restricted retirement, record pending remote retirement until lease expires, while the native receiver is immediately inactive. Never claim server received an offline revoke. Authoritative Collie paired-device revocation/expiration suppresses locally, and server device-revoke transaction suppresses schedules atomically. Invalid notification signature or connector token drops that message without retiring a valid registration (prevent attacker-induced denial of service). Temporary gateway/issuer identity expiration or unclassified 401/403 freezes HTTP-dependent enrollment/renewal/tap actions for R02 reauthentication; it does not destructively erase a valid paired bearer, retire a signed active push generation, or fabricate a server-revoke acknowledgement. A verified notification may display from its active durable registration; a tap requires current access before sensitive reads/writes. Explicit identity-device-map revocation follows its declared admission decision and any required retirement event, distinct from access-token expiry. Reinstall restores no old installation from backup.

**Tests and terminal close:** proposed `/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/test/java/com/lateapex/collie/notifications/PushEnvelopeVerifierTest.kt`, `/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/test/java/com/lateapex/collie/notifications/PushLedgerTest.kt`, `/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/test/java/com/lateapex/collie/notifications/PushRegistrationCoordinatorTest.kt`, `/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/androidTest/java/com/lateapex/collie/notifications/NativePushDeliveryDeviceTest.kt`; extend shared v1 fixtures and add `/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/test/resources/native-push-v2.fixtures.json`. Unit/bridge tests cover framing/DER/Unicode/max serialized budgets, bad signature/distributor token, wrong owner/host/reg/generation, replay/clear/restart, clock bounds, endpoint SSRF/redirect/DNS rebinding, interrupted registration and retirement, mixed gateway/issuer/Collie authentication sources and invalid-push drop without registration retirement. Device matrix API26/API33/API36: granted/denied/channels disabled, foreground/background/process killed (not force-stopped)/Doze/reboot+unlock/distributor update or removal/endpoint rotation/offline+recover/force-stop then reopen; verify scoped notification tap and zero writes, duplicate suppression, protected lock screen and key storage. Live E2E uses baseline self-hosted ntfy plus second UnifiedPush distributor/protocol conformance target as permitted by explicit operator allowlist ([official distributor list](https://unifiedpush.org/users/distributors/)); never broaden production allowlist for a test. Emit blocked/done/update/machine/cache/clear from lead and Crew targets, prove server restart persistence and paired-device revoke. Needed test deployment/certs/provider access are future acceptance dependencies, remain required and receive explicit evidence; they do not stop independent coding/test planning. A15 closes only when provider, server routes/scheduler, receiver and this E2E matrix pass, with documented OS limitations. Decoder-only green tests cannot close delivery.

Request camera only when Scan chosen; preserve manual code entry. Strict bounded URI/schema, HTTPS-only origin/mount validation, no userinfo/fragment credential import, no arbitrary intent extras/reflected URL/action. Show full new origin and mount and require explicit in-app origin confirmation before first network request; selecting a different origin cannot silently replace current credentials. Only a short-lived pairing code may be imported after origin confirmation, never bearer/browser cookie; do not log/store incoming code beyond submission. Share C27's central exact-target router for every existing/new screen (pane, History, Changes, Files/text/image with line, New/worktree, Machines, Cache, Crew, updates and supported neutral organization/admin views), not only pane links. Validate external intents in onCreate and onNewIntent; The importer, sign-in and feature Activities behind OpenLinkActivity stay non-exported.

Android HTTPS App Links require domain ownership/Digital Asset Links ([App Links verification](https://developer.android.com/training/app-links/verify-applinks)); arbitrary self-hosted domains cannot be manifest-verified generically and ADR36 excludes that deployment dependency. Mandatory closure is explicit custom-scheme/share/paste/QR import with safe confirmation, not unverified wildcard HTTPS auto-capture. Implement the required C27 verified-link addon for explicit operator build-time host lists/certificate fingerprints and operator-owned root assetlinks endpoint; this is an additive deployment mode with acceptance proving only configured hosts verify. Public APK still supplies the universal confirmed import/share/custom-scheme workflow; it never claims arbitrary runtime-host verification. Follow [intent filters](https://developer.android.com/guide/components/intents-filters).

Use WorkManager for resumable/cancelable bounded download; clear partial and revoked-origin-independent temporary artifacts. Installer gets content URI/PackageInstaller session only after explicit user Install and canRequestPackageInstalls; ask OS per-source permission only then, handle cancel/denial/no installer gracefully, never silent install or app-driven permission bypass ([PackageInstaller](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/content/pm/PackageInstaller), [install permission](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/content/pm/PackageManager#canRequestPackageInstalls()), [signing identity](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/content/pm/SigningInfo)). Manifest adds REQUEST_INSTALL_PACKAGES only for sideload channel if distribution policy permits; separate build capability hides unavailable installer workflows while showing verified download/manual guidance. Keep signing key private, out of app/repo. Current API26 target36 scope must pass shrinker/install smoke.

Actual start requires name/kind/pane_id and optional args/timeout_ms; prompt target/text plus optional wait.until/timeout; rename target/name nullable; agent.wait target/until/timeout; pane.wait_for_output pane_id/source/match substring|regex/lines/strip_ansi/timeout; events.wait match_event/timeout. Integration captures pin actual agent_prompted/agent_started/agent_info/wait/output envelopes; no fabricated one-to-one response mapping. Start scratch fake agents for lifecycle tests, real harness paid/provider checks remain scheduled acceptance dependencies. **Closed when:** all orchestration functions have native callers, bounded waits survive app recreation, occupant replacement cannot satisfy a stale wait, and uncertain delivered prompts are never blindly duplicated.

### Final review requirement — H16

#### H16 — Saved machines and remote administration through HTTP

**Depends on:** H01; H13. **Integrates with:** H17 selected remote-session lifecycle. **Current evidence:** [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/mux/registry.ts:85](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/mux/registry.ts); [/home/chris/git/collie/.project-docs/herdr/docs/next/website/src/content/docs/cli-reference.md:64](/home/chris/git/collie/.project-docs/herdr/docs/next/website/src/content/docs/cli-reference.md); [/home/chris/git/collie/.project-docs/herdr/docs/next/website/src/content/docs/connecting-machines.md:23](/home/chris/git/collie/.project-docs/herdr/docs/next/website/src/content/docs/connecting-machines.md).

**Implementation and proposed contract:** NEW host-only GET `/api/host/machines`, `/<id>/status`; POST `/plan`, `/add`, `/<id>/rename|enable|disable|remove|reconnect`. Separate SavedMachinePort uses Herdr local `machine list/status --json` and typed CLI argv; add selects explicit remote session and binds discovered SSH target/host key/auth/access route and installation/stop effects to a plan. Existing authenticated SSH/CLI/fleet routes are checked first; never conclude no access from a browser sign-out. No socket forwarded across hosts. Authentication is not forged or bypassed. Do not auto-select Local on remote failure.

**Files and native entry point:** NEW `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/host-control/machines.ts`, `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/host-control/ssh-jobs.ts`; reuse [/home/chris/git/collie/cli/ssh-config.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/cli/ssh-config.ts) and crew install logic; NEW `/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/ui/SavedMachinesActivity.kt` and native Pack linking. ##### H19 concrete rendered-history and media equivalents

Add NEW `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/media/types.ts`, `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/media/producers.ts`, `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/media/local-ingest.ts`, `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/media/store.ts`, `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/journal/rendered-recorder.ts`, `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/media/producers.test.ts` and `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/journal/rendered-recorder.test.ts`. These are proposed Collie contracts, not Herdr RPCs. Neutral `MediaProducer` has `register(SourceIdentity, AllowedTypes, Limits)`, `submit(Binding, Bytes, Metadata)` and `revoke(SourceIdentity)`; `Binding` contains owning host/runtime/session IDs, pane stable ID, current occupant fingerprint, generation, producer sequence and optional Collie observation/turn reference. Bridge generates the source handle and generation after operator-approved executable/config registration; target selection and occupant evidence come from the owning runtime. Producers cannot invent a pane owner, vendor session, semantic turn or completed lifecycle event.

The host-local ingestion contract is a NEW owner-only Unix-domain endpoint (Windows named-pipe equivalent with owner ACL), separate from mux sockets and network HTTP. Verify peer OS credentials plus a short-lived source-scoped token delivered only to the approved host-local producer; token is bound to process registration, runtime and occupant generation. A framed `submit-image` envelope carries that binding, producer sequence, declared MIME, byte count and image bytes; duplicate source/generation/sequence with the same digest returns the stored ID and differing bytes refuses. No client-supplied filesystem path is accepted. Approved journal adapters read paths only under discovered trusted journal/artifact roots, using canonical containment and no-follow file opens, then submit validated bytes through the same interface. Producer execution is a bounded argv-only host job or existing harness wrapper, never arbitrary phone command text. Register/revoke/status are typed host-control routes with H01 gates, scopes and receipts; native only receives Collie media IDs and authenticated blob URLs.

Proposed conservative limits: PNG/JPEG/WebP image bytes only, actual signature/decoder sniff agrees with type, maximum 10 MiB per image, maximum 20 megapixels, maximum 32 items per submission and 100 MiB retained per configured source/session. Refuse SVG/HTML/executable content, decompression bombs, oversized animation/frame counts and malformed/trailing-format ambiguity; decode/re-encode a bounded image before serving. Content-addressed bytes live under a server-generated private store root, encrypted at rest where the host policy requires it; opaque media IDs bind authorization to owner/session/occupant generation. Both solo and crew blob responses enforce current device admission, origin owner, MIME, bounded length and revocation. A missing Kitty-only image capture is an open producer-adapter implementation row until the registered real producer recovers equivalent visible media; there is no claim of a restored graphics RPC.

Rendered history is an explicit per-source/session opt-in policy, default off: server-generated recorder stores redacted rendered snapshots/deltas from H19 reads, Collie sequence/time, authoritative occupant generation and associated approved media IDs. Configure retention/byte ceilings, redaction rules and excluded regions before recording; disclose that secrets can occur in terminal output and provide pause/delete. Prompt/composer bodies, credential challenge data and unredacted secrets are excluded by policy; redaction executes before disk persistence and crew forwarding. A redaction failure stops recording. NEW typed `/api/history/recording/status`, `/plan`, `/enable|pause|delete` and read `/api/history/rendered` use H01 admission/receipts and scoped immutable chunks. Store these as **rendered observations**, never vendor conversation turns or resumable sessions. Native History distinguishes recorded observations from verified vendor journals, and its composer/semantic dimensions remain based on actual captured prompts and guarded registered actions.

Acceptance uses a disposable signed/registered producer and scratch harness: submit a real tool image, kill/replace the pane occupant, then prove stale-generation/reused-token/forged-source submissions refuse and old authorized media cannot leak into the replacement. Test duplicate digest receipts, MIME/signature mismatch, symlink escapes, decoder bombs, quotas, revocation mid-blob, crew owner masking and Windows pipe ACLs. Inject redaction failure and prove no unredacted bytes reach disk or peer; verify off-by-default, pause/delete/retention, redacted-history bootstrap/gap recovery and native source labeling. For every H12 row freeze whether each dimension is vendor-native, verified adapter-derived or this named equivalent. Closure requires implementation and evidence for the actual promised function; it never invents unavailable vendor session, journal, state or semantic capabilities.
