### Final review requirement — R01

#### R01 — Bounded search of the owning session journal

**Evidence:** The original Android report distinguishes loaded-turn Find from absent whole-log search. Existing history returns `fileTruncated` when only a capped tail was parsed in [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/journal/types.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/journal/types.ts); session discovery is adapter-owned in [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/journal/registry.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/journal/registry.ts), and existing read gates/routes are in [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/server.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/server.ts). This task closes the wider search functionality without promising unbounded terminal history.

**Depends on:** H01, H04 and existing journal adapters. Extend the adapter source contract with incremental normalized-turn search for file logs and database-backed logs; do not search only the existing capped in-memory tail. Add proposed `POST /api/pane/<id>/history/search` as a paired read with explicit lead/peer read classification. Input: literal query (1–256 Unicode characters), case sensitivity, `limit` (1–50), opaque continuation cursor. No regex, arbitrary path, global-server scope or executable query. Resolve the pane's current journal adapter/session on the owning runtime. Normalize text/tool summaries/results, apply the same effective masking as the text ultimately returned to native (including owning-peer and applicable lead masking), then match only masked normalized text. If a peer cannot execute that effective policy, scan its bounded already masked text pages at the lead and apply the remaining policy before matching; never expose raw-match hit counts. Never match raw secrets and merely mask the excerpt: hit counts would leak hidden values. Retain current-branch/abandoned semantics; span offsets are UTF-16 code-unit indices into the returned masked text, with code-point-safe boundaries. Bind cursors to connection/host/session/journal generation, query, redaction-policy revision and adapter version, and return typed stale-cursor/reset rather than matching another log. Freeze an initial file high-water mark or database read watermark; ordinary appends after it do not reset a long scan. Rotation/truncation, rewinds or edits of already scanned rows invalidate the generation; appended data appears in a subsequent deliberate fresh search. Do not keep a database transaction open between HTTP requests.

**Bounds and responses:** Stream at most 8 MiB or 250 ms of scanning work per continuation, cap a search generation at 512 MiB and 10,000 hits, and return `matches`, `nextCursor`, `scannedBytes`, `hasMore`, `coverage` (`complete`, `partial`, `limit`), `limitReason` and journal generation. File decoding uses a bounded incremental parser with a 1 MiB maximum record; oversized records produce explicit partial-coverage gaps rather than being silently skipped. Run scan/decode in a worker terminated on a 250 ms budget and check cancellation between <=64 KiB chunks; elapsed checks after an unbounded synchronous parse are insufficient. Database adapters use bounded keyset pages and interruptible worker-owned read connections; document scan watermarks for mutable records. Limit each paired device to two concurrent scans and ten continuation requests per second, with aggregate server worker quotas and typed rate limits. Database adapters enforce equivalent bounded statement/page budgets. Match records contain turn UUID, timestamp, part index, bounded text excerpt and explicit span offsets. Support a bounded matching-turn/context read by discovered turn UUID so native can navigate to a result older than the existing parsed tail. Whole-session coverage is claimed only for a completed scan; capped or reset results say so. Returned text is inert, current masking applies, and audit logs exclude queries/content.

**Files and native entry:** Proposed `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/journal/search.ts`, `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/journal/search.test.ts`; extend the adapter source contract and per-harness source modules, server/Crew route schemas and read allowlists. Extend native API/models/repository; add a “Search session” mode to [/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/ui/HistoryActivity.kt](/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/ui/HistoryActivity.kt), separate from loaded-turn Find. Cancel on scope change, label scan progress/partial coverage, and open the matching turn using its scoped identity. This is a foreground paginated read, not background ingestion or a new transcript export service.

**Closure:** Meaningful tests cover a hit preceding the old tail cap, multi-byte spans, enormous records and limits, database mutation/append/rotation, pi abandoned branches, invalid/foreign cursors, masking, Crew scope and cancellation. Controlled acceptance searches file-backed and database-backed scratch sessions with known old matches and confirms exact result navigation. R01 stays open until these contracts, adapter implementations and native workflow pass; a local Find widget is insufficient.

### Final review requirement — R02

#### R02 — Native access through an identity-aware front door

**Evidence and scope:** [/home/chris/git/collie/docs/deployment.md](/home/chris/git/collie/docs/deployment.md) defines the conforming front door and trusted proxy headers. [/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/ui/MainActivity.kt](/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/ui/MainActivity.kt) already launches external sign-in, but browser cookie state cannot authorize native HTTP. Preserve working native Tailscale access as the existing baseline. Implement a concrete supported native identity-gateway profile for deployments needing interactive identity authentication; publish its requirements and acceptance matrix instead of claiming that every arbitrary proxy already supports native tokens.

**Chosen contract:** An operator-configured OIDC authorization-code public client uses PKCE S256 and a DPoP-capable issuer/resource profile. The native app starts an external `ACTION_VIEW` browser and receives only the registered reverse-domain callback `com.lateapex.collie:/oauth2redirect` through the single exported `OpenLinkActivity` and routes the validated transaction to internal sign-in state; no embedded browser or cookie import. This follows [RFC 8252](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8252), [PKCE](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7636), [OIDC Core](https://openid.net/specs/openid-connect-core-1_0.html) and [DPoP](https://www.rfc-editor.org/info/rfc9449/). Configure issuer, public client ID, audience, redirect URI and exact allowed gateway origin through an explicit operator profile, imported with native origin review. Discovery/JWKS/token calls stay on declared issuer endpoints and use a separate credential client. State/nonce/verifier belong to one expiring connection-bound transaction; validate issuer, audience, signatures, code binding and token expiry. Send DPoP proofs on authorization-code and refresh-token requests using the same Keystore key; accept only `token_type=DPoP`. The chosen profile uses signed JWT access tokens, pinned issuer JWKS/algorithms and `cnf.jkt`; opaque tokens require a separately implemented authenticated introspection profile before being advertised, not a guess about token contents. Store identity tokens separately from the Collie pairing bearer in Keystore-backed encrypted custody. No embedded client secret.

**Gateway implementation:** Add proposed `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/native-access/gateway.ts`, `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/native-access/contracts.ts` and `/home/chris/git/collie/cli/native-access.ts`. This is an operator-run loopback identity adapter behind the existing single HTTPS front door, not another managed tunnel or exemption in Collie's dispatcher. The supported deployment explicitly routes the native API mount through TLS termination to this identity gateway, which authenticates every request before the local Collie. A cookie-only SSO gate must not reject that mount before the native gateway can inspect credentials. Operator routing replaces its authentication on this mount with the required DPoP gateway; it never exposes a direct unauthenticated Collie path. Keep browser routes under their original configured authentication. Test this exact ingress chain; merely putting a gateway behind an incompatible cookie gate is insufficient. To keep Collie's `Authorization: Bearer` pairing header intact, the explicitly documented gateway extension carries the identity DPoP access token in `Collie-Proxy-Authorization: DPoP <token>` and its proof in `DPoP`; it consumes both before forwarding. Verify `cnf.jkt`, access-token issuer/audience/expiry and proof method/URI/time/jti/ath/nonce against the configured public HTTPS scheme/authority and mounted path. The DPoP `htu` excludes query and fragment as RFC 9449 specifies; bind actual method and public path without decoding into a different resource. Derive public URL from operator configuration or the explicitly trusted front-door contract, never an arbitrary client `Forwarded`/`X-Forwarded-*` header. This header separation is a proposed Collie gateway extension; it is not asserted to be standard reverse-proxy behavior. The public issuer/client profile must actually support DPoP; provider-only browser sessions get a precise unsupported-profile response until this adapter is configured.

The gateway strips every incoming trusted identity/device header and injects only a verified mapped login and approved device identity. Operator CLI enrollment maps the issuer plus identity subject plus DPoP key thumbprint to an allowed device name; enrollment never writes the Collie pairing registry or silently approves a device. Pairing and proxy factors therefore remain independent and compose by AND. Add proposed `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/native-access/device-bindings.ts`: owner-only shared binding records include issuer, subject, key thumbprint, explicitly operator-mapped existing pairedDeviceId, active/revoked state and monotonic binding revision. The CLI confirms the mapping against the existing paired-device catalog without changing its authority. A trusted configured gateway verifies the request binding; Collie reads its own validated binding store, not an untrusted phone identity header. C21 registrations enrolled through this gateway record a server-derived ingress binding/revision. Revoking a map atomically emits a local typed binding-retired event to the notification dispatcher and retires only registrations associated with that binding; no new content is delivered, and connected native clients cancel their notifications. Offline clients process the signed clear/retirement when transport resumes; already delivered offline content cannot be remotely guaranteed erased. OAuth token expiry is transient reauthentication, never device-map revocation, and does not retire push. Direct Tailscale enrollments remain governed by their actual pairing lifecycle. Test event crash/replay, binding replacement and revoked-map versus token-expiry behavior. The gateway forwards only to the configured local Collie, preserves Origin/Host requirements, keeps authenticated browser ingress behavior separately configured, and never interprets callback URLs as arbitrary upstream destinations. Server reads do not gain terminal write authority merely because identity login succeeds.

**Native integration:** Proposed `/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/data/ProxyIdentityStore.kt`, `/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/network/ProxyIdentityInterceptor.kt` and `/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/ui/ProxySignInActivity.kt`; share C27/A20 transaction dispatch and A00/C01 scope cancellation. Use an Android Keystore P-256 DPoP key, strict JOSE ES256 encoding distinct from C21's DER signature format, and official known-answer fixtures. Prepare/refetch credentials before a write; on a gateway refusal do not transparently resend the terminal mutation. A read may retry once after successful reauthentication with the same scope. Define typed gateway error envelopes `{source:"native_gateway",factor:"identity"|"device",code,requestId}` and a gateway-owned response-source marker; the gateway removes any supplied marker, stamps its own refusal and preserves only verified local upstream Collie responses as source `collie`. Native parser binds these to the configured authenticated ingress/request generation and exact schema; unknown or inconsistent provenance freezes actions pending reauthentication/authoritative refresh without destructive pairing wipe. Collie authoritative paired-device revoked/expired errors alone trigger connection credential/content retirement. Add body/header spoofing and mixed-source 401/403 tests. Missing/expired credentials lead to the native sign-in/retry workflow with the exact failing factor; proxy login failure does not revoke a still-valid Collie bearer unless Collie itself reports authoritative pairing refusal.

**Dependencies and closure:** C01/A00 scope and C27/A20 callback infrastructure; superseding ADR for configured gateway token ingress plus documentation for deployment Variant C/B and operator enrollment. Tests cover intercepted/foreign callback, code replay, issuer mix-up, proof replay/wrong URL/method/token/hash/nonce/key, header spoofing, mount handling, expired tokens, revoked device mapping and zero automatic terminal retries. Controlled acceptance uses a disposable DPoP-capable OIDC issuer and the new gateway behind a conforming HTTPS front door: native login/read/Files/write succeed only with both required factors and fail after device-map revocation. R02 closes only with actual gateway/client implementation and this acceptance; documentation or an external browser opening alone is insufficient. Existing access routes are checked before requesting operator credentials for later deployment testing.

### Final review requirement — W01

#### W01 — Windows Crew admission, persistence and failover parity

**Evidence:** [/home/chris/git/collie/cli/crew.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/cli/crew.ts) explicitly refuses Windows Crew because it has not been checked; [/home/chris/git/collie/cli/crew-deputy.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/cli/crew-deputy.ts) shares the restriction. [/home/chris/git/collie/docs/windows.md](/home/chris/git/collie/docs/windows.md) consequently documents Windows 11 x64 standalone only. This is a server portability gap in the full plan, not a limitation Android can fix alone.

Use the existing dependency-free certificate implementation in [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/identity.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/identity.ts), pinned TLS/admission, [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/trust-store.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/trust-store.ts), [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/host.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/host.ts), [/home/chris/git/collie/cli/task-scheduler.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/cli/task-scheduler.ts) and [/home/chris/git/collie/cli/sys.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/cli/sys.ts). Do not invent an OpenSSL requirement: current identity code already uses Node-compatible crypto. Add proposed `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/private-state.ts` and `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/private-state.test.ts`: a shared private-state writer with owner-only Windows directory/file DACLs, secure temporary-file creation, inherited temp/replacement ACL verification and tested replacement/locking behavior, reused by Crew trust, pairing, push keys and checkpoint state. POSIX `0600` is not evidence of Windows access control. The official basis is [Microsoft access control](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/security/identity-protection/access-control/access-control) and [icacls](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/administration/windows-commands/icacls); validate the actual service account and read/write denial under another user.

Extend Task Scheduler lifecycle/config to preserve role/standby settings across restart; support explicit manually configured HTTPS ingress with no managed tunnel assumption. Audit certificate key generation/X509 parsing, mutual TLS server/client, SAN/pinning, clock/expiry, private-state atomicity, Windows path/junction/UNC containment, service account permissions and CLI argument quoting. Implement real equivalents for any discovered portability mismatch before changing `refuseCrewOnWindows`, `soloCrewHint`, CLI help/doctor and published support docs. Refusal removal follows a single capability/platform acceptance gate, not a blanket OS string deletion. Preserve mixed-version protocol decisions and existing quorum/deputy safeguards.

**Acceptance:** Add meaningful Windows cases to the existing identity/trust-store/crew/deputy/task-scheduler suites, with native Windows jobs in the existing Windows test lane. Run a disposable Windows 11 x64 lead, peer and deputy against Linux peers: invite/join, add, restart, leave, credential rotation, pinned-TLS refusal, update/rollback, deputy arm/takeover/lead-return race, machine/cache alerts and C21 registration/clear state. Include installed `collie.exe` and scoped Android acceptance after C19–C23 implementation. Record Windows/Bun/Herdr/build identities and DACL evidence. A Linux test using `hostFor("win32")` supplements but cannot replace this run. W01 stays open if the VM or required access is unavailable; continue platform-neutral work. The documented release rehearsal that resets a VM must use a disposable approved test target; this plan does not authorize resetting an operator VM.

### Final review requirement — W02

#### W02 — Windows build and action workflow without bash

**Evidence:** [/home/chris/git/collie/docs/windows.md](/home/chris/git/collie/docs/windows.md) documents a source-build bash dependency and unavailable Herdr action buttons. [/home/chris/git/collie/herdr-plugin.toml](/home/chris/git/collie/herdr-plugin.toml) has bash bootstrap/action argv; [/home/chris/git/collie/cli/build.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/cli/build.ts) owns transactional builds. Close the functional build/actions gap with portable Collie implementations; do not promise upstream native Windows builds of tmux/zellij/tuios that their projects do not provide.

Add proposed `/home/chris/git/collie/scripts/collie-bootstrap.ts` that resolves a validated Bun executable, checks the version contract in TypeScript, installs/typechecks/builds in the existing staged transaction, and invokes fixed CLI argv. Keep cached Unix bash wrappers compatible. Add a generated platform-specific Windows plugin manifest/payload invoking `collie.exe` for supported actions and Bun only for source bootstrap, without constructing shell command strings. Inspect the stable Herdr Windows plugin loader before enabling registration; if it cannot display action buttons, expose the same declared lifecycle actions through H15/H18's Collie HTTP/native host controls and CLI with explicit effect labels. Native functional parity is required; a Windows-specific UI omission is not repaired by making Android spawn bash.

**Files and acceptance:** Extend `/home/chris/git/collie/scripts/build-windows-payload.ps1`, `/home/chris/git/collie/cli/build.ts`, `/home/chris/git/collie/cli/build.test.ts`, `/home/chris/git/collie/scripts/build-cli.test.ts` and plugin/payload tests. Add a published Windows source-build/action support crosswalk with exact executable/version requirements. On a disposable Windows host lacking Git Bash, build into isolated outputs, freeze all eleven existing manifest action IDs (`start`, `stop`, `restart`, `uninstall`, `update`, `update-major`, `url`, `status`, `version`, `push-keys`, `push-test`) in the Windows crosswalk and exercise each through its declared CLI/native equivalent, including explicit no-TTY major-update consent and scoped uninstall effects. Exercise native `push-keys`/`push-test` equivalents without exposing private push keys or sending an unsolicited real notification; use the isolated test subscriber. Inject a failed build and verify old binary/PWA bytes survive. Verify native host controls execute the same safe verbs via the owning server. W02 closes only with a real no-bash build and complete supported action equivalents. MSI/winget/new Windows service packaging and unsupported Windows architectures were platform inventory limits, not reported supported features omitted by this app; no invented packaging parity is claimed.

### Final review requirement — H01

#### Shared implementation contract: H01 is a dependency of every new action

**All routes below marked NEW are proposed Collie contracts, not existing Herdr or Collie APIs.** Prefix every concrete HTTP request with the configured Collie mount. Native always talks HTTPS plus pairing bearer to Collie. Neither Android nor a crew link carries raw Herdr RPC, terminal endpoint bytes, SSH, a browser runtime or an arbitrary RPC dispatcher. The owning Collie calls local Herdr or documented host-local CLI primitives. The mux adapter remains host-local; saved remote administration uses a separate host-management port, and live remote panes are reached through that machine's Collie crew API.

For ordinary pane/layout operations, extend [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/mux/types.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/mux/types.ts) and [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/mux/capabilities.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/mux/capabilities.ts) with typed neutral methods/capabilities. For integration/plugin/server/persistence/machine administration, add NEW `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/host-control/types.ts`, `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/host-control/registry.ts`, and `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/host-control/herdr.ts`; publish their capabilities separately in `/api/config`. This avoids embedding a host axis inside MuxAdapter. Each adapter must implement verified semantics or an explicit named unsupported outcome. Implement matching native methods in [/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/network/CollieApiClient.kt](/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/network/CollieApiClient.kt), serializers in NEW `/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/network/ControlModels.kt`, repository methods in [/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/data/CollieRepository.kt](/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/data/CollieRepository.kt), and actual UI callers listed per task. New flags require `supportsNew(name) == true`; missing or unknown flags fail closed. Preserve legacy `supports()` compatibility for existing actions ([/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/network/WireModels.kt:44](/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/main/java/com/lateapex/collie/network/WireModels.kt)). An older peer's 404 becomes a clear unsupported-feature response, never a raw RPC fallback.

NEW `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/control/contracts.ts` owns discriminated request/response schemas, positive capabilities, maximum sizes, HTTP verb/gate, scope type and outcome classification. NEW `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/control/routes.ts` is called from [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/server.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/server.ts) through its existing RouteCaller/gate/runtime resolver ([/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/server.ts:563](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/server.ts)). Every session route resolves `(host,session)` once; pane/tab/workspace IDs must belong to that runtime. Host-only routes reject irrelevant session selectors and never accidentally target a lead's primary session. Use opaque object IDs, not user-supplied disk paths; derive plugin/config/backup paths from declared server-owned records with containment, owner checks and allowlists. Files/config additions require updating the current path-boundary ADRs rather than quietly bypassing their restrictions.

Read actions keep pairing/TLS/identity/host gates. Sensitive argv, logs, config and selections use `device-read` plus response redaction. Mutations keep both existing write factors and operator phone-policy switches, verify their current target and expected state, and audit a redacted verb/target/outcome, never prompt bodies, tokens, selected secret text or credentials. Native product confirmation names the real effects for close/remove/restore/install/stop/handoff/host scroll actions; this is UI design, not an extra conversational permission gate for writing this plan.

NEW `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/control/operations.ts` and `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/control/plans.ts` extend the durable receipt pattern ([/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/launch-receipts.ts:7](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/launch-receipts.ts), [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/worktree-receipts.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/worktree-receipts.ts)). Every mutation carries a request ID and binds device, owning host/session, capability, canonical payload hash, target identity and plan evidence. Same ID/same payload replays a recorded result; reuse with a different payload refuses. Reserve before dispatch; persist completion. In-flight/pending/unknown outcomes are typed and visible through NEW `GET /api/control/operations/<id>`. A timeout or disconnected reply is not proof that a write failed. Never automatically replay a terminal send, move, install, removal or restore after uncertain dispatch; inspect authoritative state and require a new deliberate product action where effect cannot be proven. Do not promise exactly-once remote effects across a crash before a receipt. Plan tokens expire and revalidate immediately before writes; they do not authorize unrelated targets.

Crew work is mandatory for every owning-host route: update [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/forward.ts:64](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/forward.ts), [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/forward.ts:159](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/forward.ts), [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/router.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/router.ts), [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/peer-client.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/peer-client.ts), [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/admission.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/admission.ts), [/home/chris/git/collie/CREW_PROTOCOL.md](/home/chris/git/collie/CREW_PROTOCOL.md), plus response masking. **Explicitly classify every new GET/read POST as read or device-read in both lead and peer dispatch.** The current default treats non-pane routes as write and unknown pane actions as write; do not inherit this by accident. Forward only a closed allowlist, preserve scopes/device attribution, pin mutual TLS/crew secret, negotiate new feature support, and preserve outcome-unknown behavior. Make the required protocol decision: additive optional fields with closed absence semantics where possible; bump the crew version if new streaming/operation behavior is not expressible compatibly. Existing Windows crew restrictions remain published only until the mandatory tested Windows crew extension in the companion Collie plan is implemented; the standalone Windows host paths in this plan still get native screens and honest capabilities.

H01 acceptance: fixtures test pairing/device refusal, peer gate parity, malformed selectors, cross-runtime IDs, capability absence on old peers, plan expiry, body-hash mismatch, concurrent duplicate dispatch, disconnect before/after dispatch, redaction and read classification. Verify a paired Android client against a disposable solo bridge and a disposable three-node crew before any new feature is marked closed. Existing tests in [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/server.test.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/server.test.ts), [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/forward.test.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/forward.test.ts), [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/router.test.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/router.test.ts), [/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/peer-client.test.ts](/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/crew/peer-client.test.ts) must gain meaningful cases; NEW `/home/chris/git/collie/bridge/control/contracts.test.ts` and `/home/chris/git/collie/android/app/src/test/java/com/lateapex/collie/network/ControlModelsTest.kt` must not replace the existing suites. Closure: contracts, port, adapter capability, gate, forwarding, durable operation result and native caller all exist for each claimed feature.

### Nested Herdr schemas, error and response rules

#### Appendix B — required nested schema, event and outcome implementation

Use these exact JSON pointers inside the retained schema, copy into typed local adapter codecs and fixture-test them. Do not forward schema objects into native screens unchanged.

- `#/schemas/request/$defs/PaneMoveDestination`: discriminated `tab` (required tab_id, split; optional target_pane_id, ratio), `new_tab` (optional workspace_id,label), `new_workspace` (optional label,tab_label).
- `LayoutNode`: pane leaf with optional command argv/cwd/env/label/pane_id, or split requiring direction,right/down, ratio, first and second. App limits depth/node counts; a layout command is executable argv and must pass launch policy.
- `AgentPromptWaitOptions`, `AgentWaitParams`: until array of idle/working/blocked/done/unknown and optional uint64 timeout. Reports use `PaneAgentState` idle/working/blocked/unknown. `PaneTextPoint`: uint32 row and uint16 col; `PaneTextRange`: start/end. `PaneCopySearchParams` requires content_revision, cursor,direction,pane_id,query; previous optional range. `OutputMatch` substring/regex each requires value. `EventMatch` is the closed per-event union with required target fields; do not accept arbitrary event names.
- `IntegrationTarget`: pi,omp,claude,codex,copilot,devin,droid,kimi,opencode,kilo,hermes,qodercli,qwen,cursor,mastracode,antigravity_cli,grok. Stable CLI-only Letta work remains H13/H12.
- `AgentViewFilter`, `AgentViewField`, `AgentViewSort`, `AgentViewValue`: recursive closed operators and builtin/token fields; cap recursion and count. `PluginInvocationContext`, `PluginSourceInfo`, `PluginPanePlacement`, `PopupSize`: populate only verified current context/source; cell sizes uint16 or percent pattern 1%–100%.
- Request/response `id` is a string. Error wire is `{id,error:{code,message}}`; the schema leaves code an open string, so known errors come from stable source and fixtures rather than an invented enum. Unknown codes preserve safe opaque detail plus classified Collie refusal; do not collapse all host refusal into retryable unreachable.

The complete **success response** discriminants are retained under `#/schemas/success_response/$defs/ResponseResult`; decoders validate expected variant and target before accepting a result. Existing response objects include pane/layout/neighbor/edge/move/swap/resize/zoom results, portable layout export/apply/split-ratio results, agent info/started/prompted/explain, wait/output matched, integration list/install/uninstall, manifests, plugin list/link/action/log/pane results, metadata acknowledgments, notification/title/config outcomes and `ok`. Do not infer success from HTTP 200 plus an arbitrary JSON object. H01 tests closed route response expectations against controlled actual captures.

Events are **invalidations plus authoritative reconcile**, not a replay log. Add feature-negotiated subscriptions from `#/schemas/request/$defs/Subscription` and translate `#/schemas/event/$defs/EventData`: workspace_created/updated/metadata_updated/closed/renamed/moved/reordered/focused; worktree_created/opened/removed; tab_created/closed/renamed/moved/focused; pane_created/closed/updated/focused/moved/output_changed/exited/agent_detected/agent_status_changed; layout_updated. Retain previous IDs and closed/created containers from pane_moved. `subscription_event` additionally carries pane.output_matched, pane.agent_status_changed and pane.scroll_changed typed data. Output-matched includes its read snapshot; scroll event includes offset/max/viewport; status data can include display metadata. Subscribers cannot rely on pane.read revision being nonzero. No plugin-registry/config/auth lifetime event is promised by this schema; perform explicit post-write refresh/poll/revocation checks where no event exists.

| capability-gap-analysis:25 | required | \| Structured tool-call and denial detail \| Native text cards omit some decoded structured call/image/denial context shown by the PWA. \| Enrich native tool cards with faithful typed context and distinguish denial from tool errors, while retaining text fallback. \| | B05 |
| capability-gap-analysis:26 | required | \| Journal attachment images and tool-result images \| Fields decode, but history projection/rendering drops image content. This can hide the only phone-readable copy of pi/OMP off-grid terminal images. \| Native authenticated, same-origin scoped, bounded image loading and rendering in both history and live transcript. Restore visible results, with an explicit failure/unsupported state. \| | B04, B34 |
| capability-gap-analysis:27 | required | \| Pane/workspace Changes, diffs and last-commit inspection \| No native API/screen for the implemented read-only Changes family. \| Native read-only lists/diff views, scoped to the source machine/session; preserve repository containment and server limits. Inspect an agent's work before approving or responding. \| | B11 |
| capability-gap-analysis:28 | required | \| Pane/workspace Files, text and image reads, path existence \| No native file browser or authenticated file-image viewer; terminal filesystem links are not promoted into this workflow. \| Native bounded file browser/viewer with distinct refusal and missing-route states; Files additionally requires the server's device-read authorization gate, beyond ordinary paired reads. Files remains read-only; editing is not an existing contract. \| | B10 |
| capability-gap-analysis:29 | required | \| Harness/command/shell launch targets and folder-based worktree planning \| New transports exist in Android, but screens still call legacy launch/worktree routes. \| Wire target discovery, folder choice, plan preview, base/ref and parent choice into native screens. Preserve caller-held request IDs across deliberate same-intent replay, without automatic write retries. \| | B02, B14, B15 |
| capability-gap-analysis:30 | required | \| Launcher recipe add/remove/rename; folder stars \| API/PWA feature family absent from native UI. \| Native configuration controls scoped to the target host, with the same confirmation and write gates. | capability-gap-analysis:31 | required | \| One-off command runs, preflight, recent-run history/removal \| Rich launch/run screen absent; these are not the same as sending text to an existing pane. \| Explicit run UI with server preflight and no-prompts confirmation, declared availability, intent identity and typed refusals. \| | B02, B16 |
| capability-gap-analysis:32 | required | \| Chat `sendQueuedNow` action \| Android displays queued messages and decodes declared keys but supplies no action. \| A guarded “Send now” action using the server-declared keys. Never resend queued text; refuse while a semantic dialog owns input, while offline/saved, or when writes are unauthorized. \| | B06 |
| capability-gap-analysis:33 | required | \| Persistent version/scope update dismissal \| Native Dismiss sets a local Boolean and calls digest snooze; server dismissal has different durable semantics. \| Implement `/api/update/dismiss` with version and offer/crew scope. Keep “remind me next digest” as a distinct snooze operation. \| | B07 |
| capability-gap-analysis:34 | required | \| Mux topology-latency declaration and force-refresh \| Android omits `topologyLatency`; Space refresh reads another snapshot without requesting the mux refresh floor. \| Decode and display bounded freshness where declared, negotiate supported read depth, and perform scoped `/api/refresh` before a manual Space reload. Mainly improves zellij's census behavior. \| | B01, B03, B21 |
| capability-gap-analysis:35 | required | \| Machine/cache notification preference switches \| Native settings expose three shared switches, while the PWA exposes five; machine/cache fields decode but are not editable. \| Add the two shared preference controls with clear bridge-wide scope, independently of native background delivery. \| | B20, B22, B26, B27 |
| capability-gap-analysis:36 | required | \| Machine resource summaries/history and alert thresholds \| Native Crew status is not a machine metrics view; machine alert fields are preserved but not editable. \| Native machine panels/history and declared alert controls. ## Validation and acceptance procedure

Use the assigned isolated checkout. The commands below name the current repository root; substitute the assigned checkout’s full absolute root consistently if using a worktree. Do not mix shared checkout source with isolated outputs. A command is required when its subsystem changes; explain legitimate applicability rather than treating an unavailable environment as a pass. New feature tests named in the copied specifications must exist and execute in addition to the relevant existing tests.

| Check | Working directory | Command |
| --- | --- | --- |
| native policy | /home/chris/git/collie | `bun run check:android` |
| native unit/lint/build gates | /home/chris/git/collie/android | `./gradlew --no-daemon testDebugUnitTest lintDebug assembleDebug assembleDebugAndroidTest assembleRelease` |
| documentation links | /home/chris/git/collie | `bun /home/chris/git/collie/scripts/check-doc-links.ts` |
| TypeScript contracts | /home/chris/git/collie | `bun run typecheck` |
| root lint | /home/chris/git/collie | `bun run lint` |
| Android release packaging fixtures | /home/chris/git/collie | `bash /home/chris/git/collie/scripts/android-release.test.sh` |
| integrated root suite | /home/chris/git/collie | `bun run test` |
| Crew harness | /home/chris/git/collie | `bun run test:crew` |

After local checks, build and install the controlled test APK and execute the feature-specific local/Crew/unsupported-peer, lifecycle and accessibility scenarios explicitly listed above. Device instrumentation uses `./gradlew --no-daemon connectedDebugAndroidTest` from the assigned checkout’s Android directory against a designated disposable/test device only: it can uninstall/replace app state and is not a reason to overwrite a user’s active paired installation. Physical push, actual proxy, signed upgrades and Windows/harness/Herdr scenarios require their named real controlled fixtures; emulator/source-only evidence cannot substitute.

For bridge/PWA compile checks, use the actual isolated override: `bun /home/chris/git/collie/scripts/build-cli.ts --outfile /home/chris/git/collie/.project-docs/implementation-evidence/capability-backlog/B41/collie-check` and a Vite `--outDir` under the same item’s isolated evidence/output directory; rebase both prefixes to the assigned checkout. Ordinary `bun run build` or `bun run build:cli` without output overrides targets live assets and is not this validation path.

**Terminal acceptance:** Every required unit and genuine-gap finding passes its full specification and local/Crew/device/platform/live acceptance; baseline regressions remain green. No outstanding acceptance item, stub, fallback or documentation-only claim counts as global closure.

Each changed native surface must cover loading, empty/unavailable/refusal, stale scope, wrong identity, rotation/process death, API26/current API, narrow screen, 200% font scale and TalkBack where relevant. Mutations additionally need stale/expired plan, changed occupant, denial, concurrent duplicate and uncertain dispatch evidence. Preserve before/after failures and re-review corrections. Record exact provider/vendor/runtime versions, not merely an archive version.

## Deliverables and handoff

Feature code, meaningful tests, native reachable surface, scoped contract/adapter/Crew dispatch where required, and applicable ADR/docs/Unreleased entries.

**Evidence directory:** `/home/chris/git/collie/.project-docs/implementation-evidence/capability-backlog/B41`. **Handoff:** `/home/chris/git/collie/.project-docs/implementation-evidence/capability-backlog/B41/handoff.json`. These are expected implementation outputs; they are not claimed to exist today. Rebase to the assigned checkout, then carry reviewed handoff/evidence to the integrator.

```json
{
  "backlog_id": "B41",
  "implementation_status": "implemented | partial | todo",
  "acceptance_status": "passed | pending | failed",
  "source_revision_or_patch": "commit or reviewed patch identity; include preserved baseline identity",
  "changed_files": [],
  "exported_contracts": [],
  "capabilities_and_gates": [],
  "scope_and_error_semantics": [],
  "tests_with_results": [],
  "live_evidence": [],
  "integration_outcomes": [],
  "remaining_acceptance": [],
  "access_limitations_and_attempted_supported_routes": [],
  "review_findings_and_corrections": []
}
```

Only mark closed when own requirements and all listed joint closure contributions have implemented behavior, review and passing feature-specific acceptance. Leave missing evidence honestly pending and continue unaffected work.

Missing credentials, provider/device/Windows access or exact vendor captures are pending acceptance with attempted supported routes recorded; they do not delete the work or authorize unverified claims. This brief authorizes normal in-scope local implementation/read-only investigation when assigned, not unrelated external writes or irreversible infrastructure changes.
